# Household Ledger — persistent project ledger

**Updated:** 2026-10-30 (Pacific)  
**Repository:** https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger  
**Working instructions:** [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md)

This file is the continuity record for future ChatGPT conversations. **Read it first, and append a concise entry after every Household Ledger user request, even discussion-only requests.** Keep the snapshot up to date; preserve earlier log entries.

## Mission and product direction

Household Ledger is a **calendar-first checking-account balance forecaster**, not a traditional budget tracker. It should answer: what will the main checking account balance be on each future day, which major deposits/withdrawals are included, and when might funds run low?

Product priorities:
- A clear daily calendar showing predicted ending balance and dated predictions.
- High confidence that income, housing payments, card payments, utilities, insurance and other major recurring items occur in each applicable month of a 30/60/90-day view.
- Simple, consistent inline editing of individual predicted payments and recurring schedules. Minimize screen-hopping, duplicate lists, unnecessary controls and multi-step workflows.
- Explicit separation of actual posted bank activity from future predictions. Show uncertain patterns for review instead of inventing payments or offering unwarranted certainty.
- A single canonical future ledger driving all account-balance math. Reconcile predicted-to-posted transactions and transfers without double counting.

**Scheduling decision:** Change **one** dated future payment or **all future** payments of a schedule. The arbitrary partial date-range/batch scheduling option was removed by explicit request. Preexisting stored range settings may need safe backward compatibility, not a new creation UI.

**Recurring decision:** Detect recurring items using their account, direction, merchant/identity and timing, not an identical amount requirement. Variable bill amounts and timing differences should not cause duplicates. Two alternating biweekly income amounts can be two independent four-week schedules offset by fourteen days, even with the same payer.

**Banking safety:** Preserve the current GitHub, Plaid and private Render architecture. Actual source transactions/balances remain authoritative and unchanged by editable predictions. Do not use public fictional Demo data as if it were real bank data. This repository is public: do not put actual financial records, private amounts, authentication details, or private user messages in this ledger.

- **2026-10-30 Whole-app UI/UX duplication assessment — RECOMMENDATIONS ONLY / NO APP CODE CHANGE:** Reviewed current `main` navigation and rendering sources for Home, Spending (Budget/Merchants/Purchase Sizes/Summary), Bills, Activity, Review, Settings and secondary Predictions/Trend. Ranked five potential simplifications for user approval before implementation: (1) one prioritized Review queue instead of separate categories, overdue, legacy estimates and suggested repeating sections; (2) one primary Bills workflow with Upcoming first and Repeating management secondary, preserving full predictions access and distinct one-occurrence vs recurring edits; (3) remove duplicate Next predicted bills from Spending Summary and show reporting period once; (4) standardize Activity/Upcoming/Repeating/Review terminology and remove repeated titles/obvious hints without removing critical trust warnings; (5) keep frequently used Settings visible, move learned rules, legacy exclusions and diagnostics into Advanced, and show compact configured credit-card summaries. Source-based review only; connected real-user browser visual inspection unavailable. **No functional or UI changes made, no application test runs required, no GitHub Pages/Render deployment claimed.** Docs-only PR will record this audit, with implementation outstanding and subject to a future request.

- **2026-10-29 Credit-card settlement budget policy audit — DISCUSSION, NO APP CODE CHANGE:** User confirmed that paying a credit card should never be budget spending on either the checking debit or the card credit; credit-card merchant purchases belong in their spending categories once, while the payment still reduces checking in the Calendar forecast. Read main `101d990b382236a0d049ff97ba5d9d057539a81f`: `planner.js` `cardActivityCents` and `categorySpendingCents` exclude recognized card payments, paired bank transfers and pending records. `accounting.js` identifies card payments from descriptors and matched account flows. `planner-history.js` safeguards reported external transfers. **Residual edge:** an unpaired, poorly labeled checking-to-card payment manually assigned a spending category may be counted as a categorized external outflow if not classified `card_payment` or marked ambiguous. **Separate reporting issue:** Budget's `historyInsights` filters to strictly positive spends before grouping, so posted merchant refunds (negative activity) currently do not reduce category totals; this was not changed in this policy discussion. Exact user transactions and private live deployment were not inspected. Future work only on explicit request: stronger conservative card-payment identification/exclusion and refund netting with fictional banking safety tests.

- **2026-10-28 conditional pending-withdrawal reconciliation — tested, merged, GitHub Pages published; private Render still unverified:** [PR #272](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/272) squash-merged into `main` at `b42dda8731e1bde319953d8eb37f495fbf7365cf`, final feature head `1b7b0341a05d87b040ed0ed2f4e7ed98ffed4368`. For confirmed recurring cash withdrawals, `planner-core.js` can provisionally pair a unique matching pending transaction with its original/moved due occurrence if bank-reported current-minus-available covers all pending outflows on that account. The matched occurrence disappears from the **future** ledger only while the pending bank evidence remains; its bank status remains Pending, next repeat remains, and no bank record or saved preference changes. Without sufficient available-balance coverage or in ambiguous cases, retain one conservative forecast reserve. When pending disappears without posting, the forecast reserve returns automatically; when it posts under a different ID, existing posted matching settles. Fictional tests also cover competing similar payments/independent schedules and missing/insufficient balance evidence. [Banking/forecast safety CI #40795522613](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40795522613) and [Chromium/iPhone WebKit browser CI #40795522757](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40795522757) **passed** on final feature head. - **2026-10-28 provisional pending-withdrawal reconciliation — implementation awaiting CI:** User approved matching bank Pending withdrawals against existing prediction occurrences. Added read-only `planner-core.js` provisional pending matching for saved recurring checking/cash withdrawals: require identical account/cash-out, original/edited due-date proximity, near-exact pending amount, unique occurrence and unique pending bank row, and a bank-reported checking *current minus available* gap large enough to cover **all** currently pending cash outflows on that account. Such a pending event provisionally clears just that forecast installment without ever labeling source bank data Posted; subsequent recurring cycles persist. If available evidence is missing, insufficient, or ambiguous, leave one conservative future debit; if pending vanishes without posting, due debit automatically returns; if it posts with a new ID, existing posted reconciliation takes precedence. Expose transient `pendingPaymentMatches` diagnostics; no persistence/actual bank mutation. Added fictional regression tests for covered/uncovered/unavailable balance, moved date, disappearance, posted ID replacement, amounts/dates/accounts, competing bank rows/recurrences and daily arithmetic. **Feature PR/CI, merge, Pages and private Render all unverified pending next checks.**

- **2026-10-28 pending withdrawals versus prediction reconciliation — confirmed cause, design decision; not yet implemented:** User verified the real bank withdrawal is **Pending**, while the corresponding previously postponed recurring prediction still debits the future Calendar. Source inspection confirms `planner-core.js` and `planner-transactions.js` intentionally exclude pending transactions from settled matching, so prior posted-only PRs #266/#269 cannot clear it while pending; this explains the remaining symptom, without proving whether the bank's available balance has reserved it. New intended behavior: a uniquely matched pending cash withdrawal should **provisionally link** to one scheduled occurrence using account, direction, merchant or conservative amount/date identity, without claiming Posted or clearing the entire repeating schedule. Preserve one cash reservation in the projection, not two: for bank accounts whose available balance already includes the pending debit, avoid subtracting its prediction a second time; when available is missing or pending coverage is uncertain, retain a single conservative forecast reserve and communicate uncertainty. On posting, transition from provisional to settled using Plaid pending-to-posted linkage (`pending_transaction_id`) where available; if pending disappears without posting, reinstate the unpaid forecast. Never mutate bank records, double-clear another installment, rely on equal amounts alone, or assume current/available are interchangeable. Plaid docs: pending metadata can change and checking `available` typically reflects pending outflows. **Discussion/design only: no code change or runtime tests this turn.** Next implement fictional regression cases for included/unincluded pending in available funds, pending-to-posted ID replacement, disappeared pending, competing same-amount bills, and monthly installment preservation; safety tests and PR merge required before claiming a fix. Private Render and live balance handling unverified.

- **2026-10-27 overdue-payment Review regression follow-up merged:** [PR #220](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/220) squash-merged to `main` at [`7749101039d2eefee010ada609eaa51649979e8d`](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/commit/7749101039d2eefee010ada609eaa51649979e8d), verified against GitHub main. The new test distinguishes not-posted/pending from confirmed posted activity, verifies a single carried debit and unaffected later monthly recurrences. Removed riskier client Review/forecast caches and bumped planner asset version; kept safe rendering speedups. **This is a regression risk mitigation, not independent reproduction of the user's private-bank UI**. Private Render deployment and live post-sync account behavior remain **unverified**.

- **2026-10-27 regression report — overdue expected payments after performance cleanup:** User reported that the expected-payment-not-posted check appeared broken after PR #217. Conservative fix restores full forecast cache invalidation for every saved prediction/review field and removes the independent Review-queue memoization; keeps safer calendar date indexing, lazy hidden trend chart, formatter reuse and future-row memoization. Extended existing posted-matching safety coverage for a fictional payment due yesterday with no posted bank record, pending evidence, then a newly posted matching record, checking exactly-once cash reservation and continued regular recurrence. No actual bank data, Plaid backend, scheduled rules, or persistent account state changed. PR and safety + browser test results/merge to be recorded after verification; **private Render deployment unverified**.

- **2026-10-27 performance cleanup merged and verified:** [PR #217](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/217) squash-merged at [`cab917f30fa7863613bbbad876eff7d2c69e4273`](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/commit/cab917f30fa7863613bbbad876eff7d2c69e4273), confirmed as GitHub `main`. Final feature head `6a0581872791d290d5873c957f676efe9e8bff04` passed [banking/forecast safety CI 40565283369](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40565283369) and [desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit CI 40565283686](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40565283686). Scope: skip unused long Trend SVG on Calendar; cache forecast-derived rows and Review queue across display-only interactions; avoid reforecasting on changes only to Review metadata; index ±6-day gap matching; reuse currency formatters. No change to source bank data, Plaid refresh cadence, forecast rules, or private server. Theoretical work reduction is supported by code audit, but live device timing was not independently benchmarked. GitHub Pages and private Render running version **unverified**.

- **2026-10-27 client performance audit (PR pending):** Review found no automatic periodic bank-sync timer; private Plaid sync remains user-initiated. Identified avoidable browser work: the Calendar builds a full three-year Trend SVG, recurrence-gap checks repeatedly scan all 1,095 daily points, and navigation repeats Review discovery and future-row sorting; saving Review-only recommendations also invalidates the three-year balance cache. Optimized these paths with lazy Trend SVG, date-indexed gap checks retaining the same ±6-day semantics, memoized future rows/Review queue, cached currency formatters, and forecast cache invalidation only for balance-relevant inputs. Actual transaction facts, posted matching, recurrence policy, account balances, Plaid requests, and private storage unchanged. Tests, merge, GitHub Pages, and private Render deployment require separate verification; private Render **not verified**.

- **2026-10-27 per-schedule bank matching windows merged:** [PR #210](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/210) squash-merged as `95108c0f210d5e25328add641fd416631d51fbba`, independently verified on GitHub `main`. Scheduled and bank-history recurrence editors offer optional Early days (0–14), Late days (0–14), Minimum amount and Maximum amount; each is persisted once on the original saved recurrence rule. Blank limits preserve the legacy cadence-based matching policy or unrestricted amounts. Posted bank transactions and overdue withdrawals use these same user-defined windows and match by saved bank account, payee and direction; independently saved same-payee streams can be distinguished by amount/time; overlapping ambiguous matches remain unconfirmed. One actual bank record may resolve only one occurrence. No changes to historical bank records, recurrence anchors, forecasts merely from configuring a matching boundary, Plaid, or Render. - **2026-10-27 overdue scheduled cash withdrawals fixed and merged:** [PR #208](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/208) squash-merged as `b9dc14d16845031b5d1a821a641b5b36d640b573` and verified on GitHub `main`. Bank's actual checking balance remains authoritative; a recently expected recurring cash debit (such as an ACH mortgage) without a matching posted transaction no longer vanishes as the date passes. Forecast carries its unique scheduled occurrence to the next business day, subject to a seven-day lookback and matching existing bank postings by saved payee/account/direction with short date tolerance and variable amounts. An unmatched overdue withdrawal appears in Needs review, counted in the red badge, with a one-occurrence date reschedule. Historical bank facts and future recurring schedule anchor unchanged. Weekend-adjusted Friday dates supported; no new tests per prior request. Final tested head `36c009b18aec11098290cab1ab8aa7d643c33f43` passed [banking/forecast safety 40531651574](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40531651574) and [Chromium/iPhone WebKit browser 40531651794](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40531651794). **Live private Render deployment not verified**, so observed household bank postings have not been directly checked.

- **2026-10-27 card-payment regression fixed and merged:** [PR #206](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/206) squash-merged as `589472c8c4a94e8fb090fda718f9f92e94333946`, independently verified on GitHub `main`. PR #204 had put a provisional `type='refund'` before card payment matching, so real incoming card settlements could be misclassified as merchant refunds, disturbing statement net purchases and inferred credit-card bills. Now card payment evidence is evaluated first, and the statement engine plus History summary exclude paired card payments/transfers from all purchase/refund subtotals, including provisional estimates and counts. Existing historical card payments stay visible as transfers. Final PR head `91f7f834c4de22e3e5df40b2a2817da7b1a277d1` passed [banking safety 40522043098](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40522043098). No new test cases added per user preference. Private Render deployment, and real card records after this fix, remain unverified.

- **2026-10-27 card-refund credit handling merged:** [PR #204](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/204) squash-merged as `e9619d1347596840bacaf6113796ad90173a404e` and verified on GitHub `main`. Read-only card refunds with incoming bank direction are classified as positive credit transactions when backed by refund words/provider category or a unique prior same-card merchant purchase; posted History and Calendar display `+`, and the statement engine already subtracts those refunds from purchases. Generic unproven card adjustments remain visible for review, not treated as refunds, transfers matching genuine card settlements remain separate, and provider current balances are not manually adjusted or double counted. Existing banking/forecast suite [40511914242](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40511914242) and desktop/iPhone browser suite [40511914195](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40511914195) passed on tested head `f5a327f32a7e5a3e03b799f6cfedc1c29c078c1d`. - **2026-10-25 Needs review save blocker fixed and merged:** [PR #202](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/202) squash-merged as `0cc0e3bdebb2c120979341dcb2026ad06e13047d` (independently verified on GitHub main). Root cause: prior unconditional duplicate-schedule validation prevented *every* forecast preference save when an older duplicate already existed; unrelated Needs review decisions were blocked and misleadingly labeled bank-login errors. ## UI/forecast follow-ups (2026-10-22)

- **Implemented and merged — schedule-aware history buttons:** Shared `historicalScheduleMatches` uses linked bank IDs first (even after provider classification/description changes), then account, direction, merchant aliases and unambiguous payroll phase without matching exact amounts. Calendar/History now open an existing schedule or require selection if ambiguous. Historical save guard blocks conflicting duplicate creation from already linked bank entries. Truly independent same-payee schedules remain explicitly possible.
- **Implemented and merged — monthly list wording:** “Upcoming predictions” shows the chosen account, remaining future forecast dates, and excludes already-posted bank history. It does not change the balance computation.
- **Unverified private forecast coverage:** A short list might simply reflect the remaining days of the month or selected account, but could also expose an omitted recurring bill. The public code and a user-provided screenshot cannot establish which saved private schedules exist or whether Render has the same revision. Verify privately and safely by comparing saved schedule identifiers against canonical future-ledger events and posted-cycle reconciliation; no account data in GitHub.

## Historical decisions / why the app looks this way

**Oct 15–18 — foundational app:** Early versions emphasized budgets, spending categorization and a synthetic recurrence/accuracy laboratory. Those experiments are preserved in the repo but are not the current product goal. Synthetic tests show algorithm behavior, not proven real-bank forecast accuracy.

**Oct 19 — simplify:** User chose to preserve integrations and styling while effectively restarting the UI as an automatic main-checking balance planner. Existing backup branches are described in [README.md](README.md).

**Oct 19–20 — missing essential payments:** Clear regular housing payments were not always predicted. The user requested better recurring detection, the ability to designate any historical bank transaction as recurring, and a Reviews surface for uncertain matches. A confirmed schedule must supersede a duplicate automatic pattern. Editing a prediction must never change source bank records.

**Oct 20–21 — reconciliation issues:** Variable utility payments were being counted twice when amounts differed from actual charges. Alternating biweekly pay amounts should be represented as independent schedules rather than merged by employer. Manual links to relevant historical transactions and persistent overrides should improve predictions over time.

**Oct 21–22 — useful coverage, simpler editing:** An earlier summary sometimes failed to identify already predicted major payments. The user wanted a calendar where each day displays its projected balance and expected transactions. Navigation and editors were too complex; unified expandable rows and one-or-all schedule editing were requested and merged as PR #30 and #31.

## Source of truth / engineering guide

Read the current source before making changes; old docs sometimes describe superseded screens.

- [README.md](README.md): canonical future ledger and current main architecture.
- `planner-core.js`: prediction analysis, materialization, daily projection.
- `planner-transactions.js`: confirmed schedules, one-off entries, individual exceptions.
- `planner.js`, `planner-summary.js`, `planner.css`: current UI and transaction editors.
- `planner-history.js`: historical actual transaction interactions.
- `payment-obligations.js`: card statement / settlement handling.
- `pilot/server.cjs`, `pilot/data-source.js`: existing private authenticated Plaid/backend design.
- [ACCOUNTING_MODEL.md](ACCOUNTING_MODEL.md): reconciliation safeguards.
- [DEPLOYMENT_STATUS.md](DEPLOYMENT_STATUS.md): separate GitHub, Pages and private Render checks.
- [DEVELOPMENT_ROADMAP.md](DEVELOPMENT_ROADMAP.md): older phase background, not necessarily current app scope.

Engineering workflow: inspect `main`, work on branch/PR, validate banking safety and desktop/iPhone interactions for functional edits, merge passing work, independently verify deployment if available, and update this ledger in the same task.

## Running task log (append at end for each prompt)

Use: **date — goal; decision; changes (or “no code change”); PR/commit; checks; deployment; next action.** Keep only non-sensitive decisions. Do not paste raw prompts. Don't erase earlier entries. If GitHub writes are unavailable, plainly report that the ledger couldn't be updated.

### 2026-10-22 — create cross-chat project memory

- **Goal:** Keep project intent and progress accessible even when a ChatGPT conversation becomes too long.
- **Decision:** This single ledger is the canonical project handoff; [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) instructs each subsequent chat to read and update it **for every project prompt**, including product discussion.
- **Changes:** Add two documentation files on a focused branch; no runtime or prediction code changes.
- **Validation:** Docs only. Do not imply that banking/browser tests were re-run.
- **Deployment:** No private Render deployment claimed.
- **Next:** Continue from current `main` and append the next task outcome here.


### 2026-10-22 — explain historical calendar recurrence buttons and two-entry monthly forecast

- **Goal:** Determine why two already scheduled posted utility transactions offer “Make recurring,” and why the October monthly transaction list contains only two items.
- **Findings:** Calendar historical row actions are hardcoded in `planner-summary.js:postedDay()`, unlike History's partial linked-rule lookup; History's exact-amount requirement can miss variable charges. The monthly list counts only projected, future-dated events for the chosen account (starting at the source as-of date), while prior October bank postings are displayed separately as actuals. A small future list alone does not prove incorrect forecasting.
- **Decision:** Explain the distinction, recommend stable schedule-aware historical actions and a clearer **Upcoming predictions** label; investigate possible missing scheduled future events only with private/authorized forecast data.
- **Changes:** Documentation only; no runtime, forecast or bank-state modifications. No new recurring schedule was created.
- **Verification:** Read `main` versions of `planner-summary.js`, `planner.js`, `planner-transactions.js` and `PROJECT_LEDGER.md`; private Render deployment and private recurring-rule data unavailable for verification. No browser or banking tests run for this docs change.
- **Next action:** With explicit request to fix, implement guarded schedule-aware buttons, forecast-range labeling, and browser regressions; validate on fictional fixtures and separately verify Render.


### 2026-10-22 — safe historical schedule actions; clearer upcoming predictions; coverage design assessment

- **Goal:** Prevent the Calendar and History screens from offering duplicate recurring plans for payments already covered by a schedule; clarify month counts; assess a simpler approach to major-payment coverage without introducing another inference system.
- **Decision:** Both historical surfaces should use one shared schedule-identity lookup. First trust explicit bank transaction links (even after provider renames/type changes), then check account/cash direction and saved merchant aliases, **never an exact dollar amount**. A uniquely matched payment opens its existing schedule; several plausible matches require a choice; an unmatched payment may be planned. Stale historical forms must refuse to create a second plan when the source is already linked. Deliberately separate schedules remain possible for independent bills/paycheck phases.
- **Code (PR [#34](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/34)):** \`planner-transactions.js\` shared \`historicalScheduleMatches\` + protected \`defineRecurring\`; \`planner.js\` Calendar/History click routing and selection dialog; \`planner-summary.js\` posted-day button and renamed **Upcoming predictions** list with remaining forecast dates, account and posted-history distinction; \`index.html\` script cache refresh. No provider/source-data mutation or forecast math change.
- **Regression tests:** Variable utility amounts, bank-record renames/reclassification, duplicate prevention for stale forms, independent same-payee four-week payroll, ambiguous schedule choice, and a History reopen flow. **Both required PR checks passed**: private pilot safety and desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit browser workflow on `f368657ec9844209cb8b5123628393652920418e`. PR #34 was merged at `495b930720ad88750f99f0ccc1dbe5c896613aee`. These checks do not verify private Render.
- **Coverage (#3) — recommendation only, NOT implemented:** Replace several overlapping heuristic mortgage/category/coverage checks with a **user-confirmed Essential payments roster** sourced from existing saved schedules (not an independent detector). Show a simple month-by-month status for each confirmed commitment: **Already posted / Upcoming in forecast / Skipped or not due / Needs attention** using its actual schedule ID and bank links. Surface uncertainty when matching ambiguous, never mark it verified or invent new transactions. Onboarding can suggest likely major items from history once, but the user confirms each commitment; the ongoing assurance derives only from confirmed plans and canonical ledger entries. Hide or retire competing heuristic coverage summaries after validating this replacement, only with explicit user approval.
- **Deployment:** GitHub commit/PR is distinct from GitHub Pages and the private Render service; check them separately. No private Render verification claimed.
- **Next:** If requested, design a single compact Essential payments roster and inspect which existing coverage panels can be removed, without adding forecast transactions automatically.

### 2026-10-22 — one lowest balance over a fixed three-year window; retire major-payment checks

- **Goal:** Eliminate 30/60/90/120/180/360-day selectors, display one lowest modeled balance across a long/infinite-feeling period capped at three years, and remove the confusing Major Payments assurance UI until an understandable alternative is designed.
- **Product decision:** The Calendar and forecast use one fixed **1095-day (about three years)** modeled horizon. The header and Summary period selectors are gone; the Summary foreground shows one lowest amount and its date, which opens that day in Calendar. No month-by-month Major Payments checklist, coverage matrix, colored 'covered/missing' assurance status, or duplicate payment-review count is shown in Calendar. The optional graph is labeled a three-year illustration. An explicit caveat notes that future pay/bills vary and unscheduled everyday purchases are omitted, so the low value is **not** a guaranteed safe-to-spend minimum.
- **Technical change:** Extend fixed/inferred weekly recurring date generation and configured card autopay loops to the same long horizon, avoiding their prior 60/110/18-cycle truncations. Keep the single canonical bank-safe projection, independent schedules, individual overrides, read-only Plaid source transactions, and historical postings unchanged. Existing category and mortgage coverage implementations may remain in source for now but no longer render in the Calendar; **do not reintroduce that UI without user direction**.
- **Validation:** PR #36 passed its final two checks (pilot safety and desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit browsers) and was merged on 2026-10-22 at `81979dc17bd6a2be807d578f7d7061f835b3c023`. The tests confirm code behavior on fixtures, not a private live deployment.
- **Follow-up decision (not implemented):** Reconsider essential-payment completeness as a small user-confirmed roster referencing **existing schedule IDs** and canonical prediction events, not a separate heuristic category/mortgage confidence engine. Do not add this yet.
- **Deployment:** GitHub merge, Pages publishing and private Render revision require independent verification. Never treat a green test as proof of the private banking deployment.


### 2026-10-22 — chart-only horizons and raw reported current

- **Goal:** Provide six independent Balance Trend views (10, 30, 90, 180, 360 days and 3 years) while showing the actual Plaid-reported current account balance directly beside the unchanged 3-year projected minimum.
- **Decision:** Trend selection is a view-only redraw of existing canonical daily balances. It cannot change forecast materialization, saved schedules, the selected Calendar day or the fixed three-year headline minimum. The trend defaults to three years when the Summary is freshly rendered. The current value comes exclusively from the read-only snapshot `account.current`, **not** `account.available`, the projection opening balance or guessed transactions. Missing `current` displays "Not reported"; Demo is labeled as sample data.
- **Files:** `planner-summary.js`, `planner.css`, `index.html`, and browser/smoke tests. No bank state writes or changes to the core forecasting engine.
- **Validation:** [PR #37](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/37) passed the required Real-account pilot safety and desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit browser checks on head `97cf52caa944d9ad28914b077a3da89b547c83a0`, and was merged to `main` as `c76006b42768ef51d94df266cb9155a85837ac50`. Tests include current vs available bank balance and all six trend ranges; earlier test-only assertions were corrected before final success. Private Render deployment and GitHub Pages publication remain unverified.
- **Next:** Preserve distinction between bank-reported current, potentially different available cash, and forecast lows when improving balance explanations.

Schedule cards no longer repeat the merchant when title and merchant are identical or show meaningless three-year occurrence counts. Settings also duplicated forecast assumptions already explained on Calendar and used two panels for banking status.

### 2026-10-22 — remove heuristic same-merchant section and number schedules

- **Request:** Reconsider the "Multiple payments to the same merchant" Schedules section and give each saved schedule an easy human-readable number starting with 1.
- **Decision:** Remove the same-day/same-merchant bank-history discovery panel and its "Create separate schedule" prompts; it is speculative and can encourage conflicting schedules when bank records have legitimate same-merchant repeats. Preserve the **actual safeguards** and independent saved schedule identities, the main **+ New schedule** action, History's schedule-aware editing, and each card's **+ Separate bill** option. Simplify the New Schedule heading.
- **Numbering:** Show a subdued numeric label **1, 2, 3…** on every schedule card, following the existing saved `futureRecurringRules` array order, and use the same reference in linked-schedule choices/edit dialogs. Labels are **view-only ordinals**. Existing canonical `rule.id` values and rules are unchanged. Numbers are deterministic across a refresh as long as saved order is unchanged, but **renumber after a schedule is removed or list order changes**; they are not permanent IDs. If immutable human references become necessary, that would require a separately approved persisted numbering design.
- **Code:** `planner.js` eliminates duplicate-detection UI, removes now-unreachable handler, adds an ID-to-ordinal display helper and schedule numbers to cards and linked choices. `planner.css` styles the identifiers; `index.html` refreshes browser asset versions; `tests/planner-browser.cjs` checks ordered consecutive numbers and two saved same-merchant plans remaining independent.
- **Safety:** No new bank writes, no changes to Plaid state, forecast arithmetic, recurring schedule creation logic, duplicate prevention or reconciliation. Remove only the heuristic *presentation*.
- **Verification and deployment:** [PR #40](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/40) passed both required GitHub checks on final head `c8cee1b5df2eed520e5bac87e07e1e9bf3cdc4a2`: **Real-account pilot banking-safety checks** and **Browser interaction test** (desktop Chromium and iPhone WebKit), including independent same-merchant payments and numbered schedule cards. Merged to `main` as `3201286e2cb7e80a19610bfd56a59e0be5a4404a`. Private Render deployment was not independently verified; do not claim live availability from the GitHub merge.

Each collapsed schedule has a single full-width clickable row displaying its existing 1-based list number, name, account/cadence, next date, amount, and estimated/fixed distinction; opening the row expands the same inline schedule editor and dated payment overrides as before. A separate keyboard- and touch-accessible **⋯** menu retains **+ Separate bill** and **Remove schedule**. Removing a schedule still requires the existing second confirmation and now offers explicit cancellation inside the menu. Display numbering continues to derive from saved rule order; internal rule IDs never change.

Existing per-occurrence and all-future editor, historical date-range exceptions, independent same-merchant schedules, and double-count safeguards remain.
- **Files:** `planner.js`, `planner.css`, `index.html`, and `tests/planner-browser.cjs`. Tests check row height/density and single-column alignment on desktop and mobile, existing edit opening, separate-bill creation through More, and two-step remove/cancel without data loss.

The regression suite covered compact single-column row heights, schedule numbering, inline edits, independent same-merchant payments, separate-bill creation via More and two-step removal with cancel. Merged into `main` as `1ad3447286acc0a83011df624e270ae4f113ff94`. The private Render deployment remains **unverified**, so a GitHub merge is not proof this UI is live.


### 2026-10-22 — schedule categories, amount sorting, filtering and category totals

- **Request:** Sort compact Schedule rows by value or category, assign a category to every schedule (e.g. Subscription, Insurance, Utility, Mortgage), add categories directly on the Schedules page, show category totals when grouped, and ensure the category **persists on every predicted transaction generated by that schedule**.
- **Decision:** Keep saved-order numbering as before, even when sorted/grouped. Add compact Sort (original, amount descending/ascending, category), Show category filter and inline **+ Category** input. Predefined categories include Uncategorized, Mortgage, Rent, Utility, Insurance, Subscription, Loan, Transportation, Healthcare, Childcare, Groceries, Tax, Income, Savings, Transfer and Other. Custom categories live in the **encrypted forecast preference data** as `predictions.scheduleCategories` (not historical bank categories or a new database); capped and validated. Legacy schedules are shown as **Uncategorized** until assigned; future edits preserve assigned values. Category is editable alongside the schedule defaults. Explicit duplicate/split schedule creation carries its category.
- **Canonical data requirement:** `planner-transactions.js` stores a validated `category` in every saved `futureRecurringRules` rule and copies it to every generated event or transfer. `planner-core.js` carries that field through every derived projected account event, including both sides of linked transfers; individual dated edits preserve the category without changing bank history. Category groups show **average monthly forecast outflows, deposits, and transfers separately** from canonical, dated ledger events in the next 365 days, divided by 12, rather than misleadingly summing weekly and monthly face values or double-counting transfers. Value sort follows the next visible payment amount (not an unrelated total).
- **Safety:** All category edits are forecast metadata only. Do not write categories back to Plaid or historical transactions, change balances, create extra schedules, or change existing double-count/reconciliation rules. Category creation does not rerender or discard an unsaved schedule editor; changing sort/filter prompts before dropping unsaved edits.
- **Code/tests:** `planner.js`, `planner-transactions.js`, `planner-core.js`, `planner.css`, `index.html`, browser regression tests and `tests/planner-schedule-categories-smoke.cjs`, required in both banking and browser CI. Synthetic fixtures verify saved categories, category propagation into dated ledger/daily projection, transfer legs and individual overrides, amount sorting, custom category creation and correct group totals.
- **Verification/deployment:** [PR #42](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/42) passed both required checks on final head `3f51c974ca2b60e538a9187e4d57b54b33842e73`: Real-account pilot banking-safety checks (including category propagation into dated predictions and transfers) and the desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit browser interaction suite (category creation, amount sorting/filtering, grouped monthly totals and schedule editing). Merged to `main` as `e04a5cfc19ede9f7545bdf425162d82bf4126d13`. Private Render deployment remains **unverified**, so do not claim the private banking site is updated.


### 2026-10-22 — next simplification priorities (design discussion; no app code change)

- **Request:** Recommend next Household Ledger product improvements after shipping compact categorized, sortable Schedules and three-year calendar-first forecast.
- **Assessment grounded in `main`:** The Schedules page already supports category sort/filter and custom category creation, but assigning a category still requires opening the full schedule form. The compact list lacks a text search. `planner-transactions.js` and `planner-core.js` contain the actual/forecast posted-coverage reconciliation and identity protections, which remain worth testing against variable bills and alternating paychecks. The earlier heuristic "Major Payments" assurance panel was deliberately retired; it should **not** be reintroduced under another name. The deployment-version menu text and prior ledger entries are not proof that private Render has the current commit.

Use forecast event identities, preserving individual payment edits, account separation, and incoming/outgoing/internal transfer distinctions. ### 2026-10-22 — UTC/Pacific balance-date correction

- **Issue:** Connected checking account balance was marked future-dated in the evening Pacific despite a valid bank observation, blanking the Calendar. Bank account disconnection is neither necessary nor appropriate.
- **Root cause in source:** `pilot/server.cjs` timestamps a successful Plaid balance refresh with an ISO UTC instant. `pilot/data-source.js`/`planner.js` use local calendar day for snapshot `asOf`, but `planner-core.js` formerly compared it with the UTC timestamp date prefix. After 5 PM Pacific (during daylight saving), a valid same-day balance can look like tomorrow.
- **Fix:** In `planner-core.js`, convert full ISO timestamp to the browser-local date before the freshness comparison; preserve date-only backwards compatibility and retain future-dated warnings for genuinely future dates. Add a Pacific-evening regression and a true-future timestamp control to `tests/planner-core-smoke.cjs`. Do not change Plaid connection, account deletion, source transactions, current balance or the forecast ledger math.
- **Status:** Target PR, CI and private Render revision need independent verification; no live bank data accessed. Future work: verify the private deployed code and actual restored Calendar without requiring relinking.

### 2026-10-22 — monthly-low display stale after month navigation

- **Bug:** `planner-summary.js` initially rendered the monthly minimum correctly, but `updateMonthLow()` searched for `[data-summary-month-date]` while the actual clickable date had `[data-calendar-month-low]`. Changing months updated calendar cells but left the minimum amount and date unchanged.
- **Fix:** Use the existing clickable date selector in the update function. A browser regression calculates the minimum from all visible calendar daily amounts in November, then confirms both displayed metric and highlighted date match. No bank state or forecasting math changed.
- **State:** PR/browser validation and private Render deployment require independent verification.


### 2026-10-22 — corrected reversed monthly minimum comparator

- **User evidence:** iPhone Calendar screenshot showed multiple days with clearly lower balances than the date marked 'Monthly low'. This was not a timezone issue or stale UI.
- **Confirmed root cause:** `monthLow()` reduced over `calendarContext.points` using `b.balance<a.balance?a:b`, which returned the **higher** point when a smaller one was found. Thus the metric and day highlight selected the monthly *maximum*.
- **Fix:** Reverse ternary to `b.balance<a.balance?b:a`, retaining the same projection series and highlighting selected minimum date. Add a targeted smoke assertion with rising balances (minimum first day) and retain browser comparison to visible calendar values. Do not touch bank data, schedules or forecast arithmetic.
- **Verification:** GitHub checks and actual private Render deployment must be independently confirmed.

### 2026-10-23 — research-backed new-user usability recommendations (discussion only)

- **User goal:** Preserve current working minimal Calendar and powerful recurring forecasting but make the product self-explanatory and easy to edit for someone new. Asked for four researched recommendations; no authorization to implement.
- **Current-source audit:** `index.html` has Calendar / Schedules / History / Needs Review, while All Predictions and Settings live under More; `planner-summary.js` shows forecast numbers and selected-day payments with inline action triggers; `planner.js` supports both one-occurrence and all-future edits, schedule forms, posted-history-to-forecast actions, and separate cash-cushion vs red-highlight settings. New users must infer the distinctions and discover some actions.
- **Ranked proposals (NOT implemented):** (1) give Calendar an immediate plain-language explanation of current bank value versus estimated future balance, plus one subtle, dismissible first-use hint; (2) make all editable predictions obviously tappable and put one consistent in-context editor behind their rows, with clearly labeled one-vs-all recurrence impact, cancel/undo and save feedback; (3) align navigation and labels to user goals, exposing common actions (add one payment, add recurring payment, edit a date) at their point of use while reserving More for genuinely advanced settings; (4) turn forecasting confidence into a restrained, actionable explanation of what future transactions are included, estimated, missing or awaiting confirmation, with a direct resolve action, clear actual/predicted distinction and no implication of certainty.
- **External design evidence reviewed:** Nielsen Norman Group research/guidance on recognition over recall, clickable signifiers, progressive disclosure, contextual onboarding, system-status visibility, user control, and small qualitative usability tests; GOV.UK error guidance. These are design hypotheses grounded in established evidence, **not** usability results measured on this app.
- **Validation proposal:** Test with approximately five first-time, nontechnical people on phone and desktop. Give realistic tasks: interpret balance, change one future bill, change all future occurrences, confirm a recurring payment from History, locate an uncertain or missing payment. Record unassisted task completion, wrong-path attempts, time, and comprehension of actual vs forecast; iterate before making large structural changes.
- **Implementation/deployment:** No app source changed, no live Render deployment initiated; docs-only PR for continuity. Prior verified working Calendar styling from PR #99 remains baseline.

### 2026-10-23 — Consistent History recurring actions and safe unscheduled opt-out

- **User feedback:** Bank activity shows “Undo Not recurring” on some rows but gives no equivalent “Not recurring” decision on other unscheduled rows. Action availability and meanings are hard to infer.
- **Decision / UI:** Every History row has its own primary button (Plan repeating, Edit plan, or Choose plan), a plain recurring-status label (No repeating plan, Automatically predicted, Auto predictions off, or Recurring confirmed), and only the safe secondary action for that state. Unscheduled rows now offer **Don't suggest** (prevent *future automatic* repeating suggestions), auto-predicted rows **Stop auto predictions**, excluded rows **Allow predictions**, and confirmed/ambiguous scheduled payees direct the user to the exact saved plan instead of offering a dangerous broad stop. No nested buttons inside role=button containers. Clear History hint explains actual bank records are read-only.
- **Safeguards:** New `excludeHistorySuggestions` action writes only an account/direction/payee-specific forecast preference. It refuses to exclude when any confirmed plan for that source or linked payee alias exists, before mutating state; unlike `stopRecurrence`, it never deletes a confirmed plan. Existing merchant/direction/phase-specific exclusions stay reversible; no change to bank history or Plaid, calculation, Render infra, or imported source data.
- **Tests:** New unit regression covers exclude → restore, account/direction isolation, and protection for separately saved payroll plans. Expanded Chromium/iPhone interaction coverage for all History states, no nested buttons, prospective exclusions, immediate allow reversal, and plan safety. Final CI and private Render verification still needed.

### 2026-10-23 — Replace cryptic History decisions with one explicit Auto-predict permission

- **Problem:** A knowledgeable user cannot infer why individual History rows offer different “Don't suggest,” “Allow predictions,” or “Stop auto predictions” actions. They asked why permission cannot be ON for every transaction, what OFF does, and whether a record disappears.
- **Design change:** One **Auto-predict: On / Off** two-state, explicitly labeled control on **every eligible bank-history row**, whether a repeat has been recognized or a plan saved. Consistent separate **Plan repeating / Edit plan / Choose plan** action. Inline permanent explanation: ON permits estimates only if repeating evidence exists, not a guarantee; OFF suppresses future automatically inferred payments for the same payee, account and direction; posted History rows are never hidden/edited and owner-confirmed repeating plans remain unchanged. For rare rows missing a usable payee/direction the control shows unavailable instead of applying an unsafe guess. Remove overlapping old row-level “don't suggest / allow / stop” controls.
- **Financial logic:** `setHistoryAutoPrediction` persists a targeted `historyOptOut` in the existing forecast-only exclusions model and reverses relevant exclusions when reenabled, including legacy phase/inferred-series decisions. `planner-core` applies that restriction only to *automatically inferred* events; explicit saved recurring events survive independently. No Plaid, source records, forecast amounts, bank balances, or infrastructure changed.
- **Tests added/updated:** Unsaved payee On→Off→On roundtrip, preservation of History row count and confirmed schedules, iPhone/desktop visible controls, separate UI status vs permission, real core suppression of automatic future expenses, and confirmed-utility/payroll safety. JS syntax validated. CI outcomes and actual merge SHA to be recorded separately; private Render release remains unverified.

### 2026-10-23 — Clarify Auto-predict permission versus saved repeating plan (discussion only)

- **User question:** Does setting Auto-predict ON create/save a schedule? How does it interact with Repeating, and does it duplicate transactions? They perceive functional redundancy even after standardizing the History buttons.
- **Verified source behavior:** Auto-predict ON is a stored account/payee/direction-level **permission** for the history pattern detector; it creates no `futureRecurringRules` saved schedule by itself. The engine regenerates eligible inferred future entries from posted patterns. Saving a plan explicitly creates/updates a persistent `futureRecurringRules` record with known cadence/amount/date. `planner-core.materialize` builds confirmed entries first and suppresses matched inferred entries through `ownerFor`; posted payment coverage also avoids re-counting. Same-source matching is designed to avoid duplicate forecast debits, but a poorly linked/renamed obligation or genuinely distinct same-payee streams still merit verification rather than blanket guarantees.
- **UX diagnosis/recommendation (not implemented):** The current ON/OFF toggle displayed alongside `Edit plan` is redundant/misleading because turning OFF cannot disable an explicitly saved plan. Simplify around *forecast status* and one obvious next action: `Automatically forecast — Confirm as repeating / Exclude`, `Repeating plan saved — Edit plan`, or `Not forecast — Add repeating`. Keep automatic opt-out as a secondary action when applicable, not an equally prominent toggle on every confirmed row; avoid two competing controls appearing to manage the same future payment. Preserve bank History and exact independent-paycheck safety.
- **Scope / status:** Explanation and UX proposal only; no application code, forecasting, bank source, or deployment changes. Requires explicit user request to implement. Private Render version remains unverified.

### 2026-10-23 — Decision proposal: detect repeating candidates, but forecast ONLY approved schedules (discussion)

- **User's requested direction:** Replace dual "automatic prediction" vs "manual repeating schedule" mechanisms with a single pathway. Bank History detects possible recurring transactions, offers **Yes / No** to create a schedule, prefills the likely frequency, amount, next date, account, payee and category; Yes should save one editable recurring schedule, No should dismiss the proposal without deleting or modifying the bank transaction. Saved schedule is then shown as **Repeating — Edit**.
- **Verified current implementation:** `planner-core.materialize` appends `detected.automatic` to future entries independently of `futureRecurringRules`, then uses `ownerFor` matching to suppress inferred duplicates if a saved rule owns the merchant/stream. Thus the existing UX reflects genuinely separate forecast creation paths, despite deduplication. Current `planner-history.draftFor` and `planner-transactions.defineRecurring` already provide proposal defaults and durable saved-plan creation.
- **Recommended target architecture:** Pattern detection produces **suggestions only**, not future ledger debits/credits. Only explicitly approved `futureRecurringRules` generate recurring forecasts (preserve intentionally added one-time transactions and existing credit-card/payment obligations per their explicit governing rules). Accept Yes with reasonable editable defaults; No records a reversible dismissal, not a bank deletion. Keep History read-only, show whether suggestion is pending, dismissed or tied to an existing saved schedule, and prevent competing schedule creation for the same historical stream. Preserve independent same-merchant streams such as alternating paychecks and explicit linked transfers; test account-aware matching and posted-settlement coverage.
- **Migration and safety are crucial:** Existing confirmed recurring plans must remain untouched. Preexisting engine-inferred, unsaved future payments must be surfaced prominently for approval/rejection **before switching the sole forecast source**, with a coverage warning or guarded transition so mortgages/income/utilities don't silently vanish and create deceptively high balances. Never auto-promote all inferred patterns as confirmed without consent. Do not overwrite real Plaid facts, user-edited prediction exceptions, bank connection or Render state.
- **Scope this turn:** Design approval/analysis only; **no application code changed** and no claim of migration, new test results or deployed feature. Next action: plan/implement a tested staged migration of inference-to-suggestion-only with yes/no scheduling and safe continuity checks; private Render deployed SHA remains unverified.

### 2026-10-23 — Step 1 of 2: permanent Recommended repeating in Needs review

- **User directive:** Split the single-approved-schedule conversion into smaller releases because previous comprehensive attempts timed out; implement now, starting with a permanent **Recommended repeating** section that never hides ignored suggestions.
- **Implemented Step 1:** Scanner `planner-history.recommendedRepeating` deliberately bypasses prior ignored/excluded/saved filters and retains old evidence rather than only a short recent shortlist. `Needs review` includes a dedicated, always-rendered **Recommended repeating** section with every discovered suggestion, saved-schedule/ignored/pending status, an **Yes — review schedule** action (existing bank-aware prefilled editable form), **No — ignore**, and **Undo ignore**. A cumulative `predictions.repeatingRecommendations` catalog is saved in the existing secure forecast settings so previously seen suggestions remain available after banking history changes; `repeatingRecommendationDecisions` persists ignored flags without hiding items or deleting real bank records. Approved plans remain listed with Edit. No duplicate independent proposal cards on the same Reviews page.
- **Safety and scope:** Step 1 deliberately does **not** alter the current automatic forecast engine, bank balances, user-approved schedules, paychecks, Plaid linkage, or Render infrastructure. Yes opens a prefilled confirmation form; no rule is saved until the user submits it. No/Ignore flags only the review recommendation at this stage, rather than silently removing projected obligations before a coordinated migration. Existing auto estimates are explicitly labeled unchanged on screen.
- **Testing:** Added history-scanner tests for ignored, excluded, approved and older recommendations; desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit interactions for persistent ignored status, reversible flags, fixed list length, bank-history immutability and prefilled schedule. Require safety and browser CI before merge; private Render revision remains separately unverified.
- **Step 2 (NOT yet implemented):** After confirming the complete candidate inventory, turn the engine's inferred recurring entries into **suggestions only**, with a visible coverage-transition warning/review for previously auto-estimated obligations so none silently vanish. Only saved approved recurring schedules should drive recurring future balances; preserve explicit single future transactions, transfers, credit-card payments and independent same-merchant paycheck streams. Do not treat an ignored record as deleted.

### 2026-10-23 — Step 2 implementation: guarded saved-schedules-only forecast

Derive currently active unsaved streams per canonical ledger, preserving separate 28-day payroll phases. `planner.js` merges active streams into the permanent Recommended repeating inventory, including missing old evidence, identifies which currently affect balances, blocks cutover while any are unreviewed, and exposes a single deliberate switch once each has been scheduled or ignored. Remove separate History Auto-predict Exclude/Restore controls, persist demo/Real forecast mode with existing adjustments, and refresh script assets. Existing ignored items remain visible; ignore alone does not remove legacy forecasts before final cutover. Bank facts and source balances are never modified.
- **Guardrails:** A saved repeating schedule always supersedes inferred same-stream estimates; independent same-merchant streams are retained. No automatic promotion of unsaved suggestions. Explicit one-time payments, transfers and card-payment processing still use existing canonical rules. The initial state is legacy **only until the guarded opt-in cutover**, to avoid secretly changing a live user's cash forecast.

### 2026-10-24 — Whole-architecture audit: simplify internals without changing website behavior

- **Goal/decision:** Audit the live bank-to-forecast data path, duplicated logic, runtime costs, CI/test overlap and old modules. **No app feature, data, source banking record, persistence architecture, UI, or forecast calculation changed.** Recommend a behavior-locked sequence of small refactors, not a backend rewrite or mass code deletion.
- **Report:** [ARCHITECTURE_SIMPLIFICATION_AUDIT.md](ARCHITECTURE_SIMPLIFICATION_AUDIT.md) includes grounded file/line evidence, risk priorities, target pipeline, the preserved user-visible behavior contract and acceptance gates.
- **Findings:** Current live flow is Plaid sync/private encrypted server → client data-source normalization → reconciled derived actuals → `HouseholdFutureLedger.materialize` → linked card obligations and 1,095-day projection → existing UI. Repeated materialization on renders/handlers, unindexed potentially quadratic posted/cross-bank matching, per-row rescans for category learning, several overlapping historical recurrence recognition layers, and old non-loaded forecasting modules warrant an audit-first simplification. The existing separate encrypted forecast write is a deliberate safety optimization; preserve it.
- **Tests:** Tree contains 86 CJS test files; five configured non-deployment workflows list 96 test invocations of 47 unique scripts, with 29 named in multiple workflows. This is workflow configuration overlap, not per-PR runtime proof. Keep conservation, banking immutability, recurring/posted dedupe, legacy settings, and Chromium/WebKit behavior coverage. New tests were **not** executed for this documentation-only audit; no benchmark or speed claim.
- **Next:** First freeze exact behavior with synthetic golden cases and instrument compute stages; then streamline test matrices, index the expensive matching paths and introduce safe snapshot-level revision caching. Unify shared recurrence/account/date primitives, and only finally retire unreachable legacy code following consumer and migration tests.
- **Deployment:** GitHub source audit only; existing private Render deployed SHA and live release remain unverified.

### 2026-10-24 — Phase 1 implemented: frozen 3-year forecast outputs and profiling

Four wholly fictional fixed-date cases freeze canonical event IDs/dates/amounts, per-day account balances through 1,095 days, reconciled actuals, ignored/suggested recurrence inventory, independent four-week payroll, posted coverage, refunds/transfers, issued and estimated card statement and Calendar metrics, pending data, and conservation/immutability. Future PRs fail the Real-account pilot safety workflow if these baseline outputs change. An opt-in test mode prints current candidate signatures; it is not used to overwrite the locked golden reference.

At 920, normalize/reconcile 41.0 ms, recurrence scan 39.9 ms, card obligations 7.3 ms, full materialize/project/audit 44.3 ms and projection alone 3.2 ms in one un-warmed run. Stages overlap, readings are noisy, and there is no claimed optimization or speed improvement.

This is a deterministic fictional-output contract, not proof of live-bank or private Render equivalence. ### 2026-10-24 — Phase 3: indexed bank matching and revision-aware projection reuse

- **Goal:** Reduce backend/browser data-processing duplication and cost while keeping all user-facing website functionality, forecasts, bank integration, saved state and financial behavior identical.
- **Implementation:** [PR #143](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/143), branch `perf/phase3-indexed-forecast-cache-20261024`. `accounting.js` now visits only opposite-direction bank rows in a ±5-day date index instead of comparing every historical row to every other row; it preserves the original index-order/score-tie ambiguity rules. `ledger-data.js` constructs one grouped learned-category safety index per reconciliation instead of scanning the entire source for each row with a learning rule, preserving individual category lookup behavior. `planner-transactions.js` indexes future occurrences by confirmed recurring-rule ID before posted-payment matching, preserving original candidate order, separate payroll phases, explicit links, ambiguous reviews and one-to-one suppression. `planner.js` reuses a derived 1,095-day forecast across tab changes and repeated view requests, but recomputes when the bank state/transaction list, starting balance/snapshot, as-of date, in-place prediction edits, account definitions, category overrides or category learning change. Real mode refresh/mode changes explicitly invalidate the cache.
- **Coverage:** The existing frozen `tests/fixtures/architecture-golden.json` was **not modified**. Extend `tests/planner-recurrence-integration-smoke.cjs` to assert that tab navigation does not rebuild the three-year forecast and that saved schedule or bank-balance changes do invalidate it. All 45 financial/security/reconciliation tests and desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit browser checks remain required before merge. Do not claim actual percent speedup without matched benchmarks on a fixed workload; these are complexity and invalidation improvements.
- **Safety and release:** No changes to the appearance, UI strings, card-statement math, canonical forecast policy, Plaid ingestion/secrets, server storage, live connected bank facts or API behavior. Verify final green CI and GitHub `main` merge before announcing completion. Private authenticated Render running SHA/status **not independently verified**.
- **Next:** If these checks pass, Phase 4 can consolidate shared account/merchant/date primitives and remove obsolete intermediate processing while maintaining the same golden-output lock. Benchmark warmed repeated workloads in both desktop and mobile before making quantitative performance claims.

### 2026-10-24 — Business-day prediction dates and five-day Calendar

- **Request:** All *future predicted* Saturday/Sunday transactions should count on the **preceding Friday** in the canonical balance model and credit-card statement estimates, not merely be hidden; Calendar should show only Monday–Friday with stable month-grid height. Avoid a rolling date-offset that drifts future recurrence dates.
- **Design:** `planner-transactions.js` exposes pure `forecastBusinessDate(occurrenceDate,asOf)`. The forecast in `planner.js` explicitly enables Friday posting through the `planner-core.js` materialization option, leaving the original unmodified/frozen calendar-day model reproducible for historical accounting baselines. Every generated occurrence retains its original `id`, `_seriesDate` / `baseDate`, cadence and source bank history; just its forecast posting date changes after owner overrides, before `payment-obligations.js` statement/charge computation and canonical `project()` daily balances. Linked transfer legs always use the same effective date. This is a forecast assumption, not rewriting actual Plaid records or issuer contractual due dates.
- **Cutoff edge:** If the prior Friday is not later than `asOf` (already covered by the actual bank snapshot), the next Monday is used rather than posting into already-authoritative history or deleting the future charge. This is a bounded exception; recurrence anchors do not move.
- **Five-day UI:** `planner-summary.js` renders 30 Monday–Friday cells in six equal rows with dated weekdays only, omitting weekend actual dates from the Calendar (still available read-only in Bank activity). `planner.css` sets five equal columns while retaining fixed day heights/two previews; script/style cache versions changed in `index.html`.
- **Regression:** New `tests/planner-business-days-smoke.cjs` checks Sat/Sun event posting, weekly and monthly independent cadence, Sunday across a month boundary, card statement inclusion, estimated autopay before a Sunday due date, equal linked legs, verified balances and unchanged historical sources; added to mandatory financial suite. `tests/planner-browser.cjs` asserts exactly five weekday headers, 30 fixed cells, stable height across 4/5/6-week months and zero visible weekend cells in Chromium/iPhone WebKit.
- **Outstanding:** CI and merge verification pending; no changes to actual records, frozen reference data, encryption, linked Plaid tokens, private Render configuration or secrets. Private authenticated Render deployment remains **unverified**.

### 2026-10-24 — Zelle repeating payment not visible on Calendar (implementation awaiting CI)

- **Report:** A saved recurring Zelle-style payment shows a next date of November 1 but appears absent from the Calendar. The report concerns private real financial data; no bank transactions/account values were accessed or copied.
- **Confirmed behavior:** Sunday November 1, 2026 moves to Friday October 30, 2026 in the canonical business-day forecast, and the five-day Calendar hides Saturday/Sunday. It therefore belongs to **October**, not November. An unchanged recurrence anchor of November 1 is correct and essential to avoid shifting future monthly payments.
- **Additional root-cause code defect:** `planner-core.materialize` previously discarded **all** single-account `type:'transfer'` events before projection, including older explicitly saved monthly Zelle/ACH external cash payments with `cashDirection:'out'` or `'in'`. This caused a real missing payment beyond the month-boundary discovery issue. The branch normalizes only those cash-account, direction-confirmed, unpaired transfer **events** to forecast-only debit/credit events after posted-payment matching. Paired internal transfers still use the separate balanced movement plans; bank facts/recurring IDs remain immutable. Ambiguous directions/unverified accounts remain omitted with a visible warning, never guessed.
- **Visibility and navigation:** Repeating's Next date editor retains the stored anchor but shows **On Calendar: Oct 30** for weekend adjustments; updating its date updates the preview. Expanded plans with a canonical occurrence offer **View [date] in Calendar**, choosing the correct account (outgoing side for linked transfers), opening the correct month and selecting the forecast day. If no future occurrence is modeled, show a concise warning instead of offering an invalid jump. For old unpaired event-transfer schedules with unknown in/out direction, expand More options automatically and offer an explicit External payment direction field; user can save a known outgoing/incoming direction to restore future predictions, while account-to-account transfers remain linked.
- **Regression checks added:** `tests/planner-business-days-smoke.cjs` asserts a fictional 11/01 direction-confirmed outgoing and incoming transfer are recorded on Friday 10/30 and recur independently 12/01; without cashDirection it is not guessed, but a warning is produced. `tests/planner-browser.cjs` asserts five-day November 1 is absent; Repeating shows Oct 30, maintains November 1 recurrence anchor, previews Friday and jumps directly to the payment on the correct selected Calendar account/day. A further browser regression seeds an old ambiguous one-account transfer, confirms **Not forecast** and an open direction selector, then saves Money out and confirms the Friday forecast appears. Existing 46 financial/bank and desktop Chromium + iPhone WebKit suites must pass before merge.
- **Status:** Feature branch, PR/checks/merge pending. No live bank/Render access or confirmed production revision; this code-level fix is not proof the user's actual Zelle rule has a particular type. No Plaid secrets, bank records/balances, verified actual history, recurrence anchors or Render infrastructure edited.

### 2026-10-25 — Scheduled category subtotal correction (PR #196)

- **Problem:** Group totals previously represented only the next full forecast month. A positive repeating schedule could display $0 (notably a November 1 payment adjusted to October 30 by Friday-posting logic). Reassigning the schedule appeared not to change group sums when its next-month contribution was zero.
- **Fix:** `planner.js` categories now show `Total shown` — the sum of one displayed amount per visible saved rule, separated as Out / In / Transfers. The row, category subtotal, sort value and instant-search subtotal use consistent amounts; group changes recompute immediately after saved category reassignment. Direction-unknown external transfers are explicitly `not forecast` rather than inventing cash direction. `index.html` cache-busts the JS. Actual forecast/calendar payment dates and amounts, account balances, source bank records, server/Plaid infrastructure are unchanged.
- **Checks:** `tests/planner-browser.cjs` covers group count, category reassignment to Other and back, responsive live search and November 1 Sunday-to-Friday behavior on desktop Chromium and iPhone WebKit. Real-account safety and browser GitHub Actions triggered on [PR #196](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/196); final pass/merge still to be verified. Private Render deployment remains unverified.

### 2026-10-25 — Review recommendation could not be reconciled

- **Observed:** User screenshot of Needs review showed a forecast-save error about a pre-existing identical schedule, incorrectly shown under 'Bank connection needs attention' with a 'Reconnect bank' action. On the previous release, the encrypted forecast endpoint validated the entire schedule catalog for global uniqueness on every write. An older duplicate prevented a completely unrelated recommendation decision, Ignore/Undo, or background review catalog update.
- **Implementation:** `planner-transactions.js` adds differential validation of durable record identity pairs against the previous forecast snapshot; refuses newly added duplicate scheduled or one-off records without blocking unrelated edits when an unchanged older duplicate already exists. `pilot/server.cjs` performs that comparison inside its serialized encrypted write queue (not before the queue). `pilot/data-source.js` distinguishes save failures from bank authentication errors and does not suggest reconnecting for forecast validation. `index.html` refreshes the transaction-module cache.
- **Safety:** No deduplication, deletion or changes to existing bank transactions, existing schedules, forecast amounts, credentials, persistent disk, or Render configuration. User did not request new tests; existing banking safety and desktop/iPhone browser CI will be used. Live deployment is not assumed from a GitHub merge.

### 2026-10-27 — Audit posted card-refund credit signs (pending validation)

- **Expected behavior:** A posted merchant refund on a credit card is a positive historical transaction; it reduces that card's bank-reported debt, and its amount is subtracted from period purchases in the estimated next statement/autopay. These are **not** checking deposits and must not be applied a second time to the already-reported card current balance. Card forecast debt arithmetic correctly represents a refund as a negative debt change, distinct from a positive historical credit entry.
- **Finding/fix:** `accounting.js` previously converted ambiguous unmatched card credits into transfers. Explicit returned charges retain refund semantics even if initially imported as transfers; bank-coded transfers and genuine card settlements remain distinguishable. A single unique same-card prior merchant purchase also provides refund evidence; remaining unclassified credits stay in review rather than changing statement amounts; no changes to immutable provider records. Merchant refunds cannot be accidentally paired with an equal unrelated internal transfer. `index.html` bumps accounting script version. Existing `payment-obligations.js` already subtracts refunds from period purchases; posted Transaction History and historic Calendar present them with a plus sign.
- **Safety/verification:** No new tests added per user preference; existing real-account safety and desktop/iPhone browser CI to run on feature PR. Deployment to private Render and user bank records not verified or accessed. No infrastructure/account-link edits.

- **Safety refinement during validation:** Existing regression checks correctly required a generic unlabeled card adjustment not to be silently counted as a refund. Narrowed the fix to bank-direction-confirmed returns with refund metadata/text or one unique matching prior merchant purchase. Ambiguous credits remain positive bank history with needs-review status but are excluded from refund statement arithmetic until confirmed. Kept legacy fictional direction-unknown records unchanged rather than rewriting pinned forecast assumptions.

### 2026-10-27 — Restore card bill forecasts and statement totals after refund-priority regression

- **Root cause:** [PR #204](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/204) prioritized generic `t.type === 'refund'` ahead of recognized card payment descriptions in `accounting.js`. The Plaid live adapter provisionally labels all incoming card credits as refunds unless a limited transfer-name pattern catches them. This incorrectly turned genuine card settlements into spending refunds, so card payment matching and provisional bill generation could fail, and statement totals could include card payments. Last working refund-first ordering was in earlier main before PR #204.
- **Fix:** `accounting.js` recognizes card payment/auto-pay evidence first; incoming card refunds need refund language, provider refund code or unique same-card prior merchant match rather than a provisional type label. `payment-obligations.js` centralizes card activity filtering and excludes card settlements/paired transfers from posted, estimated and future-to-due statement calculations; `planner.js` statement History subtotals/counts use that same purchase/refund-only distinction. `index.html` busts caches. Previously bank-reported card/current balances are not recomputed from posted activity, existing categories/schedules/transactions remain unchanged, and checking payments do not count twice. No new test cases added as requested.
- **Validation status:** Feature PR and existing banking/forecast integrity CI + desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit browser CI pending. No live private Render deployment confirmation; merge should not be reported as live rollout.

Corrected the prior refund-first error from PR #204: provisional incoming card credit types no longer override real payment descriptors. Cross-account card-payment pair recognition remains intact for inferred future card bills, and `payment-obligations.js` applies a single purchase/refund-only filter to posted statement, provisional, pending, future and card-debt estimates. `planner.js` statement net charge and count explicitly exclude card settlements, while retaining the actual payment row.
