### 2026-10-27 — Overdue scheduled payment matched against actual posting

- **Problem:** The canonical future ledger filtered out a confirmed recurring mortgage once its expected date was at or before today. An as-of-today actual balance already excludes unposted withdrawals; dropping yesterday's still-unpaid mortgage made next-day low forecasts too high. Same-day/monthly processing and Friday weekend forecasts also require a date tolerance rather than assuming exact posting dates.
- **Implemented:** `planner-core.js` examines recent saved recurring withdrawals on cash accounts, checks actual posted nonpending bank records by the same saved schedule/account/direction and a short timing window (weekly 2 days, others up to 7; variable amounts allowed), and carries an unmatched expected occurrence once to the next business day, honoring an explicit one-occurrence postponement. Saved recurring anchors and past actual bank balances are untouched. Existing matched postings suppress carryover, preventing double counting. A Friday posting date from a weekend recurrence anchor is covered as well. `planner.js` adds a counted Needs review section with original expected date, amount and a direct one-payment-only reschedule form; `index.html` cache-busts changed client JS. No bank data writes or recurrence-all-future edits.
- **Scope:** Automatic temporary carry is limited to recently overdue (7-day lookback) explicitly saved cash withdrawal rules. The source balance remains the actual bank value; reserve appears in future-day projected balances. An unconfirmed missing deposit is not assumed. Longer historical missing bills are not automatically replayed because stale multi-month recurrence assumptions could distort the forecast.
- **Checks/deployment:** No new tests added per prior explicit request. Existing safety and desktop/iPhone browser checks to be verified on PR. GitHub and private Render deployment status must be verified independently; no changes to Render/Plaid infrastructure or secrets.

The canonical forward ledger carries one recent unposted expected cash withdrawal into the next business day; a matching posted payment clears it automatically, with account/direction/merchant identity and a short timing tolerance. The Needs review counter and date-only editor let the user reschedule that one occurrence without altering the recurrence anchor or actual bank balance. No bank/Plaid/storage infrastructure changes.
- **Verification:** Final code branch head `36c009b18aec11098290cab1ab8aa7d643c33f43` passed [existing banking/forecast safety 40531651574](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40531651574) and [existing desktop/iPhone browser 40531651794](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40531651794). Per user preference, no new tests were added.

Private Render running commit and real bank posted/unposted records have not been independently verified; compare service SHA with GitHub `main` and confirm live mortgage is reflected in projected next-business-day balance.

### 2026-10-27 — Configure payment posting time and amount ranges per schedule

- **User request:** Make a saved recurring payment match actual bank postings inside editable timing and amount boundaries, avoiding missed/false matches and helping distinguish same-payee streams.
- **Implementation:** `planner-transactions.js` introduces validated optional `matchDaysBefore` / `matchDaysAfter` (0–14) and `matchAmountMin` / `matchAmountMax` (nonnegative dollars; minimum cannot exceed maximum), saved directly on the single canonical `futureRecurringRules` record. Blank date values preserve pre-existing cadence-specific and same-month matching rules; blank amounts remain unconstrained for variable payments. `postedCoverage` applies configured matching to posted transactions; matched posted entries remain one-to-one with expected occurrences. `planner-core.js` applies the same limits to outstanding withdrawals, using them to identify an unambiguous posting among same-payee schedules, and extends overdue rollover lookback through a configured late-posting window. `planner.js` exposes the four inputs in compact schedule More options and a collapsible section of bank-history/review schedule creation. `index.html` version-busts changed JS. Existing scheduled date/amount, historical bank facts, future estimates and bank balances never change merely by editing matching limits.
- **Safety:** No separate matched-transaction data store, no automatic deletion, no change to private bank API/Plaid credentials or Render. Ambiguous overlapping windows leave the payment in forecast rather than guessing a match. Existing safety and desktop/iPhone browser checks pending. Per previous explicit preference, no new tests added. Live deployment unverified.

## 2026-10-28 — Whole-product usability and reliability assessment (discussion only)

User requested a ranked five-item review of feature simplicity, data-saving design, quality, reliability and intuitiveness. Inspected `index.html`, `planner.js`, `planner-summary.js`, `planner-coverage.js`, `planner-transactions.js`, `pilot/data-source.js`, `pilot/server.cjs`, `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE_SIMPLIFICATION_AUDIT.md`, `CI_TEST_PLAN.md`, `ARCHITECTURE_BASELINE.md` and deployment notes on GitHub main. Findings: current Calendar already offers inline one-occurrence/all-future edits; Review differentiates overdue unresolved debits from suggestions; a canonical 1,095-day forecast and coverage logic exist; private bank source records stay server-authoritative; forecasts are stored separately as encrypted atomic, serialized writes; failed forecast saves reject with error, and client rollback exists. Recommended next: (1) visible account freshness + 30/60/90-day coverage with uncertainty and repair actions, (2) simplify/prioritize Review items without changing reserved unpaid debits, (3) unify Calendar-first editing and hide redundant advanced UI until needed, (4) clear saved/pending/error and reversible preference recovery with concurrency guards, (5) guide credit-card payment setup, making unknown obligations explicit. No code or private data changed; review was static source inspection, not a live usability study or performance benchmark. No tests needed for documentation-only note. GitHub docs PR/merge to be recorded via git history; GitHub Pages and private Render deployment unverified and not required for documentation-only assessment.

## 2026-10-29 — Category creation and automatic classification explained (discussion only)

- **Request:** Explain where to add a custom category and what determines automatic/guessed categories in the current app.
- **Verified in source:** On **Bills**, the `+` next to **Category** opens **New category → Add**. The shared registry persists custom definitions in private forecast preferences (`predictions.scheduleCategories`) and makes them available across Bills, Bank History and Review; adding a name alone does not categorize bank records. Bank History per-row category selection stores a private transaction override; **Things to check → Categories to confirm** handles missing/Other/Uncategorized and uncertain assignments for posted transactions and saved bills. Known income and transfers may use their transaction kind in reporting without altering source facts.
- **Current classification:** The incoming Plaid personal-finance category/confidence is mapped to broad Ledger categories when supported, with conservative recognizable-merchant and mortgage rules as fallbacks; multi-purpose merchants are intentionally not guessed from their merchant name alone. Confidence under 0.7, missing categories, and merchant-match guesses remain reviewable. An explicit per-transaction correction takes precedence and does not change the read-only bank transaction or cash-flow math.
- **Limitation / possible future enhancement:** `ledger-data.js` defines learned merchant/category rules, but no call to `learnCategory` was found in the active editing UI (`planner.js` and page modules). As implemented, editing one transaction does not establish a guaranteed category rule for future purchases. If implemented later, favor explicit opt-in and cautious handling of mixed-use merchants.
- **Work/checks:** Static inspection of `AGENTS.md`, `PROJECT_LEDGER.md`, `index.html`, `planner.js`, `ledger-data.js`, `categorization.js`, `bank-feed.js`, `pilot/server.cjs`, and page modules at current GitHub `main`; **no application code, bank data, forecasting logic or infrastructure changed**. This is a documentation-only request; runtime/banking/browser tests not rerun. Public Pages and private Render versions **not verified** in this inquiry. The documentation change is recorded via the corresponding GitHub PR/commit history.

## 2026-10-29 — Learn category corrections by merchant (implementation; verification pending)

- **Request:** Make manual category corrections help categorize future purchases automatically, while keeping Amazon, Costco, Target and other mixed-use merchants review-first.
- **Behavior:** Bank History and **Things to check → Categories to confirm** now save the transaction-specific category override alongside a conservative learned merchant/category rule. Scope is the same bank account, exact normalized merchant identity and expense direction. Learned rules apply only to unknown/low-confidence source categories; explicit transaction corrections win and sufficiently confident source categories are preserved. Mixed-use merchants or conflicting histories generate **review-first suggestions**, not silent automatic recategorization. Review identifies suggested category where useful. The system uses deterministic local rules, not AI.
- **Persistence/safety:** The existing authenticated private `/api/ledger/category` endpoint optionally accepts `remember:true`, derives the learned identity from server-authoritative source records, and atomically saves only private category overlays and learning metadata in the existing encrypted state. A scoped `/api/ledger/category-rule` action forgets one rule while leaving past manual overrides intact; Settings shows learned rules and **Forget rule** controls. Demo learning stays in Demo-only browser preferences. Bank transactions, Plaid account connections, balances, payment posting, forecast preferences and prediction calculations are not written by category learning.
- **Files:** `ledger-data.js`, `planner.js`, `index.html`, `pilot/data-source.js`, `pilot/server.cjs`, `tests/planner-browser.cjs`, `tests/category-learning-save-smoke.cjs`, `tests/ci-suite.cjs`, plus this ledger. Branch `feature/merchant-category-learning-20261029`. Browser safety and private server tests use only fictional data.
- **Status at implementation:** Feature and regression code committed on branch; required banking/forecast integrity and desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit workflows, merge to `main`, GitHub Pages publication, and the private Render deployed revision are **pending independent verification**. Follow-up entries must record actual CI/merge status, and must not claim private Render is deployed from GitHub alone.

## 2026-10-29 — Private Calendar classifier dependency failure (implementation awaiting CI)

- **Symptom / diagnosis:** Private iPhone Calendar displayed a JavaScript TypeError while forecasting a connected account: `Categorization.classify` was called when `HouseholdCategorization` was absent. The latest category-report-only PR #320 did not edit forecasting or this dependency. Root cause is a previously unguarded separate browser script dependency; exact reason the resource was unavailable on the live phone is unverified.
- **Fix:** Private Render server embeds the canonical `categorization.js` file inside the authenticated planner HTML response in normal script order, eliminating that one independent network request; no duplicate classification implementation. If the file or marker is missing in a partial deployment, the server returns 503 rather than a misleading bank/balance error. Ledger model now reports a specific missing-module error instead of a null dereference. Add tests for private HTML dependency loading and iPhone/Chromium behavior with the standalone categorization request blocked. No live bank data, server storage, account linkages, Plaid settings, forecast arithmetic, or categorization rules are changed.
- **Workflow:** Branch `fix/private-calendar-category-dependency-20261029` from `main` `c3f238e87b61b40d82153e88e4d1e424dde82395`. Required banking/forecast safety, desktop Chromium/iPhone WebKit tests, PR merge and private Render live deployment are awaiting independent verification.

- **Additional source-level root cause found:** `pilot/server.cjs` inserted `pilot/data-source.js` into `<head>` ahead of `accounting.js` and `categorization.js`. Because the adapter captures those globals once at load time, they remained null for the lifetime of the page, weakening Plaid income/category classification even when both files loaded. The fix moves adapter insertion immediately after the inlined classifier and after the existing accounting script, before `ledger-data.js`. New regression asserts the strict order in the authenticated private HTML. This is part of the same not-yet-verified PR #321, with CI rerun required at the new head.

The latest category-list-only report change did not directly modify the forecast engine.

Clear missing-component message protects public Demo failure path.
- **Passing checks:** [banking/forecast safety run #40939408416](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40939408416), [desktop Chromium and iPhone WebKit run #40939408414](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40939408414); browser tests run the authenticated private UI with the independent classifier request intentionally blocked, then classify fictional Plaid activity. ## 2026-10-29 — Clarify credit-card payment budget treatment

- **User question / decision:** Credit-card repayments are balance transfers, not new household consumption. Both sides of a checking→credit-card settlement should be **$0 for Budget**, while posted purchases made using the card **do** count once in spending categories; card repayments still reduce the checking-account Calendar balance when they actually post or are predicted. Example: a $500 card grocery charge and $500 repayment from checking is $500 budget spending, not $1,000 or $1,500.
- **Code audit only; no code changes:** Current `planner.js` purchase/budget counting excludes recognized `card_payment`, `internal_transfer`, linked/paired transfers, pending, and non-purchase types; `accounting.js` reconciles many bank-identifiable payments. `planner-history.js` manual external-outflow reporting excludes known card payments and card-side account activity but may admit an **unmatched** outward checking payment mislabeled by its bank and manually assigned a spending category, if payment evidence is absent. Budget currently also filters out refunds before group totals and does **not net merchant refunds into spending**; unrelated to payment settlement but noteworthy. No new scripts, permissions, connections, amounts, financial forecasts, or banking state modified. Private Render live SHA and affected user transactions remain unverified.
- **Process:** Docs-only PR on `docs/credit-card-budget-settlement-policy-20261029` from `main` `101d990b382236a0d049ff97ba5d9d057539a81f`; record CI and merge outcome later. No change to budget policy until user requests implementation.

- **Documentation completion for credit-card budget policy (2026-10-29):** PR [#324](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/pull/324) merged to main at `a78795d8275ddde9ee4d965d3c80b72de71a3d1b`; desktop/iPhone browser Action [#40944742361](https://github.com/markbobthomas/household-ledger/actions/runs/40944742361) passed at tested head `9e7f94b5abbede8c60d607b76097c1e65a6fd1df`. No product implementation changed, so no banking safety Action triggered. Previously documented potential card-settlement misclassification and excluded merchant refunds are observations only, not fixes. Render release unverified.

## 2026-10-29 — Budget settlement protection and posted-refund netting (awaiting PR checks)

- **User request:** Implement the previously recommended two Budget fixes: both checking and card sides of credit-card repayments must be excluded from categorized spending, even when a bank names the payment unusually; genuine posted merchant refunds must reduce the applicable category's spending.
- **Scope:** Reporting-only logic in `planner.js` and `planner-history.js`; no changes to raw Plaid data, reconciled accounting types, bank links, forecast projection, card statement computations or private adjustment persistence. Existing recognized card payments and paired transfers remain excluded. For an unrecognized outward checking payment, Budget searches **all posted account history** for a unique same-amount, nearby (within five days) inbound credit on a linked credit account, requiring explicit payment wording or specific bank card-payment evidence, and excludes both sides. Merely coincidental dollar amounts, multiple candidates, refunds, income, and real unpaired external bills do not qualify. The Budget category drill-down uses the same exclusion. Unverifiable payments may require transaction review rather than silently discarding real expenses.
- **Refunds:** Budget includes posted refunds as negative amounts for their resolved category in the calendar month they posted, including refunds for previous-month purchases. Existing read-only category resolution and explicit user corrections are respected. Budget percentage and progress bars clamp to zero if net spending is negative; donut chart gives no negative slices and retains negative category line items. Merchant and statement reports are unchanged by this Budget-specific fix.
- **Tests:** New fictional `tests/planner-budget-netting-browser.cjs` checks real UI with an injected card purchase, same-month return, later-month return, and unusually named checking/card settlement in desktop Chromium and iPhone WebKit; `tests/planner-category-spending-smoke.cjs` covers matching ambiguity, actual refunds and external bills. Browser regression suite extended. Branch `fix/budget-card-settlements-and-refund-netting-20261029` based on `main` `43be6e58c03611b56fc9f0e92133fd9b25b717ac`. CI/merge and live private Render deployment not yet verified.

## 2026-10-29 — Fresh first-time user simplicity assessment (recommendations, not implementation)

- **Request:** Newcomer felt overwhelmed and did not know where to begin; assess entire UX and give exactly three impactful changes that make purpose and action clear using visual design. No implementation authorized.
- **Findings from code:** Entry `Calendar` begins with account selector, current balance, monthly low, 3-year low, five-day transaction grid, and a mechanics-based date-tip, without a prominent sentence explaining the promise. Four primary destinations `Calendar`, `Bills`, `Bank History`, `Summary` sit alongside `Things to check` and a `More` menu; Budget is one of four second-level Bank History views, `All upcoming payments` is in More, and recurring Bills is not the complete forecast ledger of upcoming cash payments. Review spans category decisions, overdue alerts, unsaved legacy estimates, and repeating suggestions; card payment setup lives in Settings. These are meaningful cognitive load and discoverability risks, not defects proven by a live user study.
- **Recommendations (ranked):** (1) Human-first Home with "Will upcoming bills be covered?" visual narrative, prioritizing available today, lowest balance this month, next bill; calendar immediately below, 3-year low in details. (2) Goal-based top navigation Home / Spending / Bills / Activity; combine Summary content with Home/Spending, group Budget+merchant+purchase-size insights under Spending, keep Activity for actual transactions, show all forecasted cash withdrawals including linked card repayments in Bills, retain advanced editing/Trend/Settings via progressive disclosure. (3) Persistent but quiet contextual "Next step" / "Things to check" that prioritizes only genuinely consequential setup and review; one decision per interaction with plain-language effect and clear completion state; no forced tutorial, no data hiding, no extra forecast engine.
- **Scope:** No HTML/CSS/JS or finance logic change. Documentation PR only; original `main` SHA `a62544257b4af865381568834231228a8e022188`. No live account accessed, and no interactive user testing performed. Follow-up implementation should preserve existing features, private financial data, merged forecast ledger, and Chromium/iPhone plus banking safety checks.

## 2026-10-30 — Action-first, low-copy UI audit

- **Request:** Remove visible duplicated field labels (for example, `Category` directly over `Choose a category` in Things to check) and similar redundant headings throughout the app, without extra explanation or action steps. **Scope:** reviewed app-rendered controls and descriptions in `planner.js`, `planner-summary.js` and `index.html`. Eliminated duplicate visible captions on Review category and sort selects, Bills search/sort/category, Activity search/sort/category, Merchants search, Budget manager new category input and its `All categories` header, actual transaction category detail, Spending Summary repeated page title, Calendar `SELECTED DAY` and list `Transactions (N)` headers. Review subtitles omit `Missing category`, since the section itself provides that information. Search placeholders now describe the immediate action. Retained structural page-section names, relevant account/month identities, statements about forecast uncertainty, payment confirmation consequences and genuinely informative empty/error states.
- **Implementation:** Hidden captions stay available to assistive technology via the common `.visually-hidden` utility, controls keep explicit accessible names, and `minimal-control-label` CSS maintains toolbar alignment. Cache versions changed for styles and both UI scripts. Adjusted browser regression to test absence of visible duplicates, continued dropdown accessibility and mobile geometry. Files: `planner.js`, `planner-summary.js`, `planner.css`, `index.html`, `tests/planner-browser.cjs`. Branch `ui/remove-redundant-labels-20261030`; CI, PR, merge and deployment results will be confirmed separately. All real bank, category assignment, schedule, budget and forecast behavior stays unchanged.
